                                       ANUKRET (Sub Decree)

                                                  ON

          THE ENFORCEMENT OF THE LIST OF PROHIBETED AND RESTRICTED GOODS


                                              8
                                The Royal Government of Cambodia

   -   Seen the Constitution of the Kingdom of Cambodia;

   -   Seen Reach Kret No. NS/RKT/0823/1981 dated 22 August 2023, regarding the Establishment

       of the Royal Government of Cambodia;

   -   Seen Reach Kram No. NS/RKM/0618/012 dated 28 June 2018, promulgating the Law on the

       Organization and Functioning of the Council of Ministers;

   -   Seen Reach Kram No. Ns/RKM/0196/18 dated 24 January 1996 promulgating the Law on the

       Establishment of the Ministry of Economic and Finance;

   -   Seen Reach Kram No. NS/RKM/0707/017 dated 20 July 2007, promulgating the Law on

       Customs;

   -   Seen Reach Kret No. NS/RKT/0719/1024 dated 06 July 2019, regarding the Establishment of

       the Economic and Financial Policy Committee;

   -   Seen Reach Kret No. 43 NS/RKT dated 28 February 2022, on the Organization and Functioning

       of the Ministry of Economy and Finance;

   -   Seen Anukret No. 21/ANK/BK dated 01 March 2006, on the Trade Facilitation through Risk

       Management;

   -   Seen Anukret No.17/ANK/BK dated 26 February 2020, on the Enforcement of the List of

       Prohibited and Restricted Goods;

   -   Referred to the Proposal of the Chairman of the Economic and Financial Policy Committee.


                                                                                         Page 1 of 3
                                                                                                    (2)




                                             HEREBY DECIDES

Article 1.-

               It is put into force the Lists of Prohibited and Restricted Goods which is in line with the

existing Cambodia Customs Tariff 2022 attached in the Annexes of this sub-decree.

Article 2 ù-

               For commodities listed in the annexes of this sub-decree and intended for multi-sectoral

purposes, an export-import permit is mandatory from one of the ministries or institutions specified in

the annex. Alternatively, the permit may be issued by another competent ministry based on the

intended use or business objective.

Article 3ù-

               When there is an update on the World Customs Organization's Harmonized System

and the ASEAN Harmonized Tariff Nomenclature (AHTN), the Ministry of Economy and Finance

has the authority to issue a proclamation to revise the commodity HS codes outlined in the

annexes of the sub-decree.

Article 4 ù-

               Prohibition, requirement of a license, permits, or conditions concerning the exportation,

importation and transit of goods are set out in the annexes of this sub-decree. If necessary, the relevant

ministries and institutions may propose amendments to the lists, including the addition or removal of

commodities, and changes of the treatment of exported, imported, and transited goods. The proposal

will be evaluated by the Economics and Financial Policy Committee for evaluation before seeking

approval from the Royal Government.

Article 5ù-

               In the event of an imminent risks necessitating urgent action, the competent ministries

are authorized to impose temporary measures for controlling the importation, exportation, and transit

of high-risk goods, and shall notify the General Department of Customs and Excise of Cambodia,

Ministry of Economy and Finance for enforcement. The ministries shall delineate the dates of

commencement and conclusion of the measures, not exceeding six months. The timeframe may be

prolonged or prematurely terminated based on the prevailing risk assessment.



                                                                                                 Page 2 of 3
                                                                                                        (3)




 Article 6ù-
               The competent ministries shall fully participate in implementing of the National Single
 Window system by accelerating the process of issuing licenses or permits for export, import, or transit
 to the requesting individuals within three working days from the application date, on the condition
 that the requiring documents are accurate and complete.

 Article 7ù-
               Sub-decree 17 ANKr.BK dated 26 February 2020 on the Enforcement of the List of
 Prohibited and Restricted Goods and other regulations contradicting this sub-decree shall be nullified.

 Article 8.-
               Minister in charge of the Council of Ministers, Minister of Economy and Finance, Ministers
 of all Ministries, and Heads of Relevant Institutions shall respectively implement this sub-decree from
 the 1st May 2024 onward.


                                                                        Phnom Penh, 28th December 2023

                                                     Annex 5

                             To Anukret No. 370 ANKr.BK Dated 28 December 2023

                                   Explanatory Notes for the Enforcement of

                                  the Lists of Prohibited and Restricted Goods
I- Definition
        Prohibited and Restricted Goods are items that whose exports, imports or transit are subject to a
certain condition due to any of the following purposes:
            -    The protection of national security;
            -    The Protection of public order and standards of decency and morality;
            -    The protection of human, animal or plant life or health;
            -    The protection of national treasures of artistic, historic or archaeological value;
            -    The conservation of natural resources;
            -    The compliance with the provisions of any legislation of The Kingdom of Cambodia currently
                 in force;
            -    The fulfillment of obligations under in the United Nations Charter.
        These lists are established in accordance with the provisions outlined in Article 8 of the Law on
Customs promulgated by Reach Kram No NS/RKM/0707/017 dated 20 July 2007.
        The goods listed in these Annexes are subject to:
        -       Prohibition on import, export, or transit;
        -       Requirement of license, permit or other equivalent legal documents for the permission of the
                export, import or transit;
        -       Requirement of entails specific certificates from exporting country.
This sub decree consists of 5 annexes as follows:
        -       Annex 1: List of prohibited goods
        -       Annex 2: List of restricted goods
        -       Annex 3: List of goods required certificate from exporting country
        -       Annex 4: List of goods prohibited or required permit for international transit
        -       Annex 5: Explanatory Notes for the enforcement of the lists of prohibited and restricted
                goods.
II- Notes:
- The provisions detailed in Annex 2 of this sub-decree do not govern the export or
     import of non-commercial goods, such as samples, personal effects of passengers, items
     belonging to moving accommodation, or those of similar nature. However, this
     exemption does not extend to items presenting significant risks to welfare, security, or
     social safety.




                                                                                                       Page 1 of 4
                                                                                                    (131)




-   The regulations outlined in Annex 3 of this Sub-decree do not apply to the export or
    import of non-commercial items valued at USD 1,000 or less for customs purposes.

-   The import or export of items derived from animals or plants listed in the Convention on
    International Trade in Endangered Species of Wild Fauna and Flora (CITES) mandates a
    permit from the competent authority of the Ministry of Agriculture, Forestry, and Fisheries.


-   The following items, which cannot be included in the list of goods in Annex 1 or
    separated due to technical reasons related to the customs tariff, are also prohibited from
    import:
      • Right-hand drive vehicle;
      • Plastic bag with handle of less than 0.03 millimeter in thickness, and its bottom width of less than
       250 millimeters;
      • Used vehicle tires, batteries and accumulators;
      • Religious books, politics books, works of art, pornography, advertisement images, and other kinds
       of printed documents prohibited by laws;
      • IPR infringing goods.

-   The following items, which cannot be included in the list of goods in Annex 2 due to
    technical reasons related to the customs tariff, are also restricted from import:

      • Used computers, shoes, bags, air conditioners, refrigerators, and freezers are

       permissible with authorization from the Ministry of Environment;

      • Toy drones classified under subheading 9503.00.99 require authorization from the

       Ministry of National Defense and the Ministry of Post and Telecommunications.
-   For test equipment classified under subheadings 9027.20.00 and 9027.89.90 in Annex 2,
    which are subject to treatment codes MOH (1) and (2), the regulations apply only to those
    goods used in the health sector.
-   The export of mining products requires a license or permit from the Ministry of Mines and
    Energy.
-   If an import permit is obtained from the Council for the Development of Cambodia (CDC),

    no additional permit from the competent ministry or institution is required.

-   For general exports, if an exporter requires a certificate in a specific form as mandated by
    the importing country, the competent ministries or institutions shall issue the requested
    document, regardless of whether those commodities are listed in any annexes of this Sub-
    decree.


                                                                                                  Page 2 of 4
                                                                                                 (132)




III- Treatment Codes:
   (1): Import license, permit or other legal documents in similar forms from competent
       ministry/institution or competent authority is required.
   (2): Export license, permit, or other legal documents in similar forms from competent
       ministry/institution or competent authority is required.
   (3): Importation is prohibited.
   (4): Importation and exportation are prohibited.
   (5): Veterinary certificate or hygiene certificate of meat or animal product from exporting country is
       required.
   (6): Phytosanitary certificate from exporting country is required.
   (7): Fisheries pathology certificate or health/safety certificate on forestry/fishery products from
       exporting country is required.
   (8): Transit permit is required.
   (9): Transit is prohibited.
IV- How to read the list of prohibited and restricted goods
   1. Acronyms
               MAFF: Ministry of Agriculture Forestry and Fisheries
               MISTI: Ministry of Industry, Science, Technology and Innovation
               MME: Ministry of Mines and Energy
               MOH: Ministry of Health
               MOC: Ministry of Commerce
               MOE: Ministry of Environment
               MOI: Ministry of Interior
               MOD: Ministry of National Defence
               MPTC: Ministry of Post and Telecommunications
               MEF: Ministry of Economy and Finance
               MCFA: Ministry of Culture and Fine Arts
               MLMUPC: Ministry of Land Management, Urban Planning and Construction
               NBC: National Bank of Cambodia
               NACD: National Authority for Combating Drugs
   2. In order to determine which goods are subject to any specific treatment and which

       ministry has competency over them, it is necessary to check the annexes. For example,
       tariff line 0101.21.00 is listed under treatment code MAFF (1) (2) in annex 2, indicating




                                                                                               Page 3 of 4
                                                                                    (133)




   that the import or export of these goods requires an 'import permission' or 'export
   permission' from the Ministry of Agriculture, Forestry and Fisheries.
3. (3), (4), and (9) are the prohibition treatment codes for the importation, exportation,

   and transit of goods. However, in cases of necessity or national social and economic
   interest, stakeholders can request permission for export, import, or transit from the
   Royal Government through the relevant competent ministry.
